# Memory Vault — Build Handoff

**Current version:** V0.2 Closed Beta (BRD §6.2 — sleep consolidator, boundaries hardening, cross-device sync, 30 beta users)

**Last updated:** 2026-08-31 (session 34) - 🔧 **PACKAGING EXPOSED THAT THE APP WAS HANDING EVERY TESTER A SETUP COMMAND THAT COULD NOT RUN.** The `.mcpb` one-click bundle works (141.9 MB, live handshake against a fresh vault) - and building it surfaced five defects that months of code review had not, because all five only appear when you RUN it. The worst: the connect snippet named `vault-cli`, a binary not shipped since ADR-SEC-018, and omitted six required paths, so anyone following the app's own instructions got `command not found` on the single step the product exists for. **ADR-101** fixes the binary rather than the snippet - `zaaheen mcp serve` now resolves its own vault and models - plus the guards that would have caught it, including a test that parses the pasted line verbatim. **ADR-100** moved model acquisition down to `vault-app` so the CLI can fetch the reranker itself, concurrently with serving. Also: `get_info()` introduced us as "Memory Vault" v0.1.0 and never mentioned `memory_read`; the keychain recovery dialog named a credential entry that no longer exists. PR #50 merged, repo renamed to **`zaaheen`**, machine cleaned (old build, vault and credential removed; ADR-SEC-005 live-verified again). All gates green. ⚠️ **Nothing built yet - the fixes are source-only - and it has STILL never run on a machine that is not the founder's.**

**Prior (session 31):** — 🔴 **THE VAULT WAS WRITING MEMORIES TO DISK IN PLAINTEXT. FIXED, SEALED, AND THE LEAKED FILE IS DESTROYED ON STARTUP (ADR-SEC-007) — PLUS "DELETE EVERYTHING" NOW CRYPTOGRAPHICALLY ERASES THE VAULT AND THE APP STOPS LYING BY OMISSION ABOUT UNINSTALL (ADR-SEC-008).** **How it was found is the part worth keeping: it was not a code review.** The founder asked a product question — *"when the vault is uninstalled, does the data get removed?"* — and answering it honestly meant looking at what is actually on disk. The sweep came back: `vault.db` ✅ SQLCipher-encrypted (header is random bytes, not `SQLite format 3`), `graph.sealed` ✅, the whole `lance/` store ✅ — and **`reports/personal.report.json` PLAINTEXT, holding verbatim fact text, memory ids, `as_of` timestamps and confidences, readable with a plain `Get-Content` and no key.** BRD §11.5.1 is unambiguous: *"All data on disk is encrypted. No exceptions."* **ROOT CAUSE was a checklist-coverage gap, not carelessness:** §11.12 puts *"No plaintext data on disk, ever"* under **vault-storage**, but the REPORT is written by **vault-consolidator** and read by **vault-retrieval** — the artifact was born in crates whose checklist never mentioned at-rest encryption, so every crate was individually compliant while the vault was not. **The fix adds NO crypto (SP-5):** `seal_vault_blob`/`unseal_vault_blob` wrap the already-shipped XChaCha20-Poly1305 envelope used by the vector store and graph. **The boundary binding came free** — AAD is `BLAKE3("vault-at-rest-v1" || relative_path)` and the path is `reports/<boundary>.report.sealed`, so the boundary is *inside* the AAD by construction and the cross-boundary swap attack (§11.3.2) fails closed with no new mechanism. **Layout moved down to `vault_storage::report_layout` as a CORRECTNESS decision, not tidiness:** writer and reader are in crates that don't depend on each other, and once the path became AAD input a one-character divergence between their two copies would seal fine, write fine, then fail EVERY unseal — degrading silently to `REPORT_MISSING` with a green build. One definition removes that by construction. **MIGRATION IS THE HALF THAT ACTUALLY CLOSES THE HOLE** — the sealed writer alone would leave every existing plaintext REPORT on disk forever under its old filename, so a startup sweep re-seals and **deletes the plaintext unconditionally, including on the failure path**, and destroys leftover `.report.json.tmp` files that carry the same text. **Then ADR-SEC-008, from the original question:** researched rather than assumed — Windows Installer explicitly carves user data out of its "leave nothing behind" rule; **Signal's uninstaller DID delete user data, cost a user their entire message history, and the issue was closed "not planned"**; 1Password (our closest analogue) leaves vault caches and tells users to clear Credential Manager by hand. So **keeping data on uninstall is right; keeping it SILENTLY was the bug.** The app now states plainly that uninstalling removes the app and not your memories, shows the exact folder, and offers a **type-to-confirm "Delete everything"** that destroys the master_key — every other key is a BLAKE3 subkey of it, so that one act makes SQLCipher, Lance, the graph and the sealed REPORTs permanently unreadable (NIST SP 800-88 Purge-level, and unlike file deletion not defeated by journaling, wear-levelling, or a backup taken last week). **⚠️ THE ORDERING IS THE WHOLE DESIGN — key first, files second:** interrupted, key→files leaves cryptographically dead files ✅ while files→key leaves a live key and any surviving copy still decrypts ❌; if the key step fails **nothing is touched** and it reports failure. Erasure walks an explicit allow-list (never a recursive delete of a caller-supplied directory) and spares the 3.5 GB of models, which hold no user data. **✅ FULL DoD GREEN ON A COLD FROM-SCRATCH BUILD (5h51m):** `build --workspace` **0 warnings** 69.3m · `test` vault-storage 41.1m / vault-consolidator 60.9m / vault-retrieval 52.3m / vault-app 63.1m / vault-tauri 28.9m — **488 passed / 0 failed / 44 ignored across 39 binaries** · `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` **0 warnings** 35.1m · `fmt --all --check` clean. The 6 log lines containing "warning" were checked individually and are **test names** about health-warnings, all `ok` — genuine compiler warnings: zero. **🧰 AND A HARD-WON DISK LESSON: the founder was right and I was wrong.** He said wipe all old builds and start fresh; I pushed back to save rebuild time, kept ~90 GB of `target/`, and `cargo clean -p` then rebuilt on top of it until `target/` hit 146.8 GB and free space hit **1.1 GB** — four gates died on `os error 112` with zero test failures anywhere in the log. Full wipe + Docker prune + **vhdx compaction** (the prune reported 0 B while `docker_data.vhdx` still held **45.86 GB**; compacting returned **42.65 GB**) took the box from 1.1 GB to 173 GB, and the cold re-run went green end to end. **ALSO: ✅ UNINSTALL REMOVES THE SCHEDULED TASK — LIVE-VERIFIED PASS ON THE FOUNDER'S MACHINE. ADR-SEC-006 IS CLOSED, AND WITH IT ADR-SEC-005.** Session 30 left this code-complete and MSI-verified with only the live run outstanding; it ran this session, elevated, in 24 seconds. **The negative control is what makes the result mean anything:** uninstalling the OLD build `{7BDA7062…}` (which has no removal action) left the task **PRESENT** — proving the gap was real and that a later ABSENT is not just "the task was never there". Installing the NEW build `{1E26A76E…}` left it **PRESENT** (the `REMOVE="ALL"` condition correctly keeps it off the install path). Uninstalling the NEW build left it **ABSENT**. **Verified at MECHANISM level, not just outcome** — `msi-uninstall-new.log` shows `Doing action: RemoveMaintenanceTask` → `Return value 1`, while the old-build uninstall log has **zero** occurrences and the install log has **zero** `Doing action` hits. End state re-checked independently of the script's own reporting: task gone, install dir gone, 0 registered products, **vault data intact at 3.5 GB**. **🔬 THE SESSION'S REAL FINDING IS WHY THIS DIDN'T RUN IN SESSION 30 — AND THE RECORDED DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG.** Session 30 concluded *"the UAC prompt was never accepted"* and warned future sessions off `Start-Process -Verb RunAs`. **Elevation was never the problem.** `uninstall-verify.ps1` was **BOM-less UTF-8 containing em-dashes** (`—` = `E2 80 94`); PowerShell 5.1 loads BOM-less files as **CP1252**, where byte `0x94` is a **right curly quote `”`** — and **the PowerShell tokenizer accepts curly quotes as string delimiters.** Every em-dash silently closed its string mid-line, so `powershell.exe -File` died at parse time having executed **nothing**. Reproduced before being believed: `PSParser::Tokenize` reported 5 syntax errors, every one on a line pairing an em-dash with a `$(…)` subexpression, and both the parse and the byte-scan are clean after rewriting the script in pure ASCII. **`-Verb RunAs` worked first try this session.** The generalisable rule — **any `.ps1` preserved for a later session must be pure ASCII**, because our own prose style is hostile to PowerShell 5.1 — is now an environment note with a two-line pre-flight check. **And the deeper lesson is ADR-098's in a new costume: "it launched" is not "it ran."** Session 30 correctly observed that nothing had happened, then attributed it to the most plausible-sounding cause instead of instrumenting; a wrapper that writes a marker on its first line separates *never started* from *started and died* in a single run, and is now preserved. **Hardened while fixing:** step 1 uninstalls the old build before step 2 installs the new one, so a missing or wrong MSI would have left the founder with **no app and a void test** — the script now pre-flights elevation, MSI presence (with a fallback to the preserved copy), and **reads the MSI's `CustomAction` table over COM to confirm it is the build that actually contains `RemoveMaintenanceTask`**, aborting before touching anything otherwise. That check earns its keep: both MSIs share the filename `Memory Vault_0.2.0_x64_en-US.msi` and differ only by ProductCode and 86 KB. **CI confirmed green before any of this** (`4d0ce0e` run `30385973201` success), and the ADR-098 smoke-job fix has now held **two further unattended weekly runs** (2026-08-03, 2026-08-10) — the alarm we repaired is still working. ⚠️ **The app is left UNINSTALLED by design; reinstall before the onboarding-step-4 replay** (command in §1). **NEXT = commit this, reinstall, then onboarding step 4; DISTRIBUTION still 🔴 blocked on a domain name.**

**Prior (session 30):** 🔔 **THE WEEKLY REAL-MODEL SMOKE JOB IS FIXED. IT WAS A DOC COMMENT, AND IT HAD BEEN RED FOR SIX STRAIGHT WEEKS (ADR-098).** Founder-directed as this session's first task, and the diagnosis session 29 left behind held up — but I **reproduced it before trusting it**. The job (`real-model smoke`, `ci.yml:660`) failed every week from 2026-06-22 to 2026-07-27 while **the real-model tests inside it PASSED** (`3 passed`, 194s); what killed it ran afterwards. Root cause: the doc fence at `phi4_mini.rs:67` was tagged ` ```ignore `. The job runs `cargo test -p vault-llm -- --ignored`, and **`--ignored` selects exactly the blocks marked "don't run" and forces them to COMPILE** — so an illustrative snippet referencing `tauri_app` (which does not exist) can never compile → `Couldn't compile the test` → exit 1. **The fix is one word:** the fence is now ` ```text `. **VERIFIED BY REPRODUCTION, NOT BY READING** — with the fence reverted, the exact CI command failed locally with a byte-identical signature to the GitHub log (same `E0425`/`E0433`/`E0728`, same `phi4_mini.rs (line 67)` test id, same `Couldn't compile the test`); with the fence fixed, the same command returns `running 0 tests … ok`. **THE ONE-WORD FIX SHIPS WITH A GUARD, because this job has now been silently red twice** — `ci.yml`'s own comment records an earlier month-long stretch ending 2026-05-18. New `crates/vault-llm/tests/doc_fences.rs` walks the crate's `src/` and fails on any `ignore`-tagged fence, naming `file:line` and explaining the CI consequence in the failure message. **Negative-verified:** it FAILS on the reverted fence (pointing at `phi4_mini.rs:67`) and passes on the fix, and it carries a non-vacuity assertion so a broken directory walk cannot make it pass silently — the same class of bug it exists to catch. **A SECOND LANDMINE FOUND, LOGGED NOT FIXED:** `vault-consolidator/src/consolidator.rs:5` has the identical defect — a BRD §5.6 spec quote with `{ ... }` bodies under an ` ```ignore ` fence. It is **dormant** (no CI job runs `--ignored` on that crate), so per the no-drive-by rule it is tech debt rather than scope creep — fixing it would also have dragged that crate's 12-binary, RAM-hungry test gate into a doc-fence commit. **ALSO CORRECTED: ADR-097's heading claimed the shipped clustering gate is 0.89. It is 0.84** — verified at `consolidator.rs:208`, not inferred. The ADR body, its table and the live-run evidence were already right; only the headline lagged the founder-directed retest that changed the value. A future session reading just that heading would have drawn the wrong conclusion about a correctness-critical number. **CI for the ADR-097 commit `b615a20` confirmed `success`** (run `30300387744`, 54m26s) before any staging. **Gates: `build --workspace` 0 warnings · `test -p vault-llm -j 2` **24 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (real-model) across 4 targets, +1 test · `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` 0 · `fmt --all --check` clean.** **✅ THE JOB IS NOW GREEN — OBSERVED, NOT INFERRED:** `real-model smoke` **`completed success`** on PR #32 (run `30378255194`), its first pass since 2026-06-22. Both pushes green too (`a64f544` run `30376571861`; PR run 59m38s, every job). **🚨 AND THEN, TRYING TO RE-VERIFY IT, A SECOND FINDING: THE ON-DEMAND TRIGGER NEVER EXISTED.** The job's `if:` keys on a `run-llm-smoke` PR label — and **that label had never been created in this repo**; there was no `workflow_dispatch` either. Its condition was satisfiable **exactly one way: the Monday cron.** So the only signal fired once a week, unattended, and session 29's documented "just trigger it on a PR" would have silently matched nothing had I followed it literally. Fixed in the follow-up PR: `workflow_dispatch` added, the missing label created, and the PR carries that label so the job proves itself. **A conditional trigger is not verified until it has actually fired once.** **PR #32 MERGED** (`4d0ce0e`, rebase → linear history, branch deleted), so the job is firable on demand from now on: `gh workflow run ci.yml --ref main`. **🧹 THEN, SAME SESSION, ADR-SEC-005's LAST OPEN ITEM: UNINSTALL NOW REMOVES THE SCHEDULED TASK (ADR-SEC-006) — CODE COMPLETE + MSI BUILT + MSI TABLES VERIFIED; THE LIVE INSTALL/UNINSTALL RUN IS THE ONE THING OUTSTANDING.** The gap was not hypothetical: `\com.memoryvault.maintenance` was **still registered on the founder's machine right now** from a session-27 test — `Status: Ready`, `Next Run Time 29/07/2026 5:25 AM`, `Last Run 28/07/2026 10:05:51`, **`Last Result: 1`** — pointing at a `target\debug\vault-cli.exe` inside the repo. A task that outlived its creator by weeks, firing and failing daily, unnoticed. Fixed with a WiX custom action driving `schtasks /Delete`; **`schtasks` and NOT our own `vault-cli`, because during an uninstall our binary is the thing being deleted** (lock/absent/crash would fail the user's uninstall). **Verified in the BUILT MSI, not just the source:** `CustomAction` row `RemoveMaintenanceTask | 98 | INSTALLDIR | "[SystemFolder]schtasks.exe" /Delete /TN "com.memoryvault.maintenance" /F`, sequenced at **3499** with condition `REMOVE="ALL" AND NOT UPGRADINGPRODUCTCODE`, against `RemoveFiles` at **3500** — so the fragment was not silently dropped and the upgrade-safety clause survived into the shipped installer. **THE SESSION'S BEST FINDING CAME FROM MY OWN GUARD FAILING ON MY OWN WORK:** `tests/installer_contract.rs` failed with *"2 `<Fragment>` elements"* — because the **explanatory comment I had just written contains the literal `<Fragment>`**. Pulling that thread: `installer.wxs` documents itself heavily and its comments quote the exact strings the guards search for (the task id, `Return="ignore"`), so **three of the four guards would have passed on prose alone — the uninstall command could have been deleted outright and the suite would still have gone green.** Fixed at the mechanism, not the wording: every guard now runs against comment-stripped markup, the stripper has its own non-vacuity test, and it was **negative-verified by sabotage** — breaking the real `ExeCommand` while leaving the comment intact makes guard 1 FAIL, where before it passed. **Gates (uninstall work): `candle` clean · `test -p vault-tauri -j 2` **59 passed / 0 failed / 8 ignored** across 6 targets (+5) · `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` 0 · `build --workspace` 0 warnings · `fmt --all --check` clean.** **NEXT = 🔴 RUN THE LIVE UNINSTALL VERIFICATION (elevated, script preserved outside the repo — see item 1), then onboarding step 4, then DISTRIBUTION (🔴 blocked on a domain name).**

**Prior (session 29):** 🎚️ **THE CLUSTERING GATE IS NOW 0.84, AND MEASURING IT FIRST CAUGHT A SHIP-GATE TEST THAT WOULD HAVE PASSED FOR THE WRONG REASON (ADR-097).** Session 28 left a 0.92 → 0.89 change picked out and ready; it shipped **0.84** instead, because the founder said test the parked option rather than assume it. Before applying anything I swept the artefact the change was most likely to break — the **100-entry / 20-topic acceptance ship-gate** (ADR-045 §d floors: precision ≥ 0.95, recall ≥ 0.90) — from the diagnostic rather than paying a ~35-minute crate run. Result: **precision is a perfect 1.0000 at EVERY gate from 0.96 down to 0.80**; the move is pure recall gain, **0.9300 → 0.9650**. It also showed **0.92 was one step from failing its own ship-gate** (0.93 at the next value up vs a 0.90 floor). The real test then returned **exactly** what the sweep predicted — `precision=1.0`, `recall=0.965`, Gate A `100/100` — so the instrument is trustworthy for the next such call. **THE FINDING THAT WASN'T IN THE PLAN:** `contradiction_resolution.rs` asserted `cos < 0.92` as a **premise** — Vega/Atlas must sit *below* the gate, which is the entire reason the A5 nearest-neighbour pass exists. **Measured, that pair is 0.9049**, so at 0.89 it clusters and the premise is FALSE. Worse, naively "fixing" the number would have made it pass **for the wrong reason**: its single-response mock feeds contradiction-judge JSON to `decide_merge`, which can't parse it, so the cluster is logged-and-skipped and Phase 2b catches the pair anyway — green via a parse failure, proving nothing. Fixed properly: premises now **computed from the shipped config, never hardcoded**; a `PhaseAwareMock` answers each phase on its own schema; and a new test restores the below-gate coverage on a fixture **measured** at 0.8196 (below the gate, above the 0.70 NN floor). **Why the looser gate is safe:** `decide_merge` is already contradiction-aware (`Merge` / `KeepSeparate` / `Contradiction{clear_winner}`), anything left active is re-judged by Phase 2b, and the one path where a fact can vanish unjudged — deterministic dedup — is untouched with **`contraBLIND` 0 at every gate from 0.96 to 0.80**. Also learned: **the classes are INTERLEAVED** — contradictions at 0.9042–0.9049 score *above* paraphrases at 0.8939–0.9025 — so no threshold separates them and safety must come from the judgement path, which is where it comes from. **✅ THEN PROVEN LIVE BEFORE COMMIT — TWICE (founder-directed, and it changed the shipped value).** Run 1, fresh 14-memory vault at 0.89, real 2.32 GB Phi-4: `cluster_count=6`, 3 duplicate pairs merged, **0 of 3 contradictions merged**, 3 auto-resolved. That result retired the very reason 0.84 had been parked — *"we haven't observed the model"* — so run 2 tested it: fresh 22-memory vault at 0.84, `cluster_count=10`, **4 duplicate pairs merged, 0 of SIX contradictions merged**, 6 auto-resolved, 0 blind collapses, 0 skipped, controls untouched, and a deliberately-baited chaining trap (*"lives in London"* beside *"design studio in London"*) did not fire. **Nine contradictions handed over across both runs; zero wrongly merged.** 0.84 dominates 0.89 on every measured axis (recall 0.965 → **1.0**, one extra duplicate pair rescued, same perfect precision), so 0.84 ships. **The lesson worth keeping: "we haven't tested it" is a reason to test, not a reason to ship the weaker value.** **Gates (re-run in full at 0.84): `build --workspace` 0 warnings 12m01s · `test -p vault-consolidator -j 2` 183 passed / **0 failed** across 14 targets · `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` 0 · `fmt --all --check` clean, no `Cargo.lock` drift.** ⚠️ **Use `-j 2` for the consolidator test gate** — at the default `jobs = 4` its 12 test binaries link concurrently and drove free RAM to 0.31 GB (see session-29 environment notes). **NEXT = confirm CI, then the live-run leftovers (uninstall-removes-task, onboarding step 4) and DISTRIBUTION (🔴 blocked on a domain name).**

**Prior (session 28):** 🛡️ **THE VAULT WAS SILENTLY DISCARDING FACTS. FIXED, AND RE-VERIFIED LIVE (ADR-096).** Chasing session 27's open finding (5 of 12 same-meaning pairs never cluster) produced a **falsification** and, underneath it, a **live correctness bug**. *The falsification:* `phases/dedup.rs` documented contradictory pairs as *"score < 0.92 cosine, so they never cluster at the 0.92 gate"* — **measured across 8 contradictory pairs the class spans 0.7199 – 0.9979**, with four breaching the documented 0.883 ceiling; the 2026-05-31 calibration had only ever sampled *different-value* contradictions, never *reversed-argument* ones. *The bug that premise hid:* `"The user prefers tea over coffee."` vs `"The user prefers coffee over tea."` scores **0.9979 — higher than ANY of the 12 true paraphrases (max 0.9936)** — and, having an identical word SET, scores containment **1.00**, clearing BOTH deterministic-dedup axes. Both axes are order-blind *by construction* (BGE cannot see argument reversal; `token_containment` compares sets), and two order-blind checks cannot catch a reordering. **Confirmed live on a clean 4-memory vault: 4 facts in, 3 out** — `clusters deduped: 1` · `contradictions queued: 0` · summary markdown `## Merges` **EMPTY** and `Auto-resolved: 0`. A fact left the vault and every user-facing artefact said nothing happened. **Fix = a third, ORDER-SENSITIVE axis** (`is_pure_reordering`: same word multiset in a different order → decline the blind collapse, route to judgement). **Re-verified live on the fixed build, same 4 facts, fresh vault:** guard logged · `clusters deduped: 0` · `contradictions_auto_resolved=1` · summary now reads `**Auto-resolved (newer fact won):** 1` + per-boundary detail. The newer fact still wins — that is the INTENDED clear-winner outcome per `Memory Vault Tests.md` K6 — the difference is that it is now a **recorded contradiction** rather than an invisible deduplication. **Rode along:** dedup collapses rendered NOWHERE in the run summary (counts existed per-boundary and in `ConsolidationReport`, but `write_merges_section` rendered only LLM merges); the Merges section now carries `**Collapsed as duplicates:** N` + per-boundary detail. **Gates: `build --workspace` 0 warnings · `test -p vault-consolidator` 162 lib + all integration targets, **0 failed** (+9 tests) · `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` 0 · `fmt --all --check` clean.** **NEXT = the 0.92 clustering gate itself (session 27's original finding), which now looks TRACTABLE — see the re-opened analysis below ADR-096.**

**Prior (session 27):** ✅ **THE SCHEDULER IS LIVE-VERIFIED ON WINDOWS, AND RUNNING IT REDESIGNED THE UI (ADR-094).** Session 26's feature was DoD-green but had never been RUN. Running it proved ADR-092/093 end-to-end **and** found two things tests could not. **Proven live on the founder's box:** ticking the opt-in registered a real per-user task — `Next Run Time 26/07/2026 5:25 AM`, `LogonType=InteractiveToken` (no elevation, no UAC), `StartWhenAvailable=true` (catch-up), `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries=false` (**Windows defaults this to TRUE — a laptop would otherwise silently skip every run**), `MultipleInstances=IgnoreNew`, `RunOnlyIfIdle` absent, correct program + fully-quoted arg vector aimed at the app's own vault. "Run now" spawned the bundled `vault-cli`, which opened the live vault and returned a real report **while the desktop app was open** — ADR-093's subprocess model works and the concurrency came out clean. Phi-4 acquisition fired on opt-in and hash-verified 2.32 GB in 135s. **BUG 1 (UX → ADR-094):** the founder set a time, pressed **Save schedule**, and nothing was scheduled — the opt-in checkbox was unticked, so the command correctly ran `unregister`. *The backend was right; the UI was a trap*, and the **silence in the log was the evidence that found it**. Fixed by the founder-directed redesign: **Maintenance → Consolidation**, three numbered cards (turn it on / when it runs / run one now), each with a plain-English explainer, and "Run now" moved to its own card with its **own** status line. **BUG 2:** onboarding read **"STEP 2 OF 3"** and **"STEP 3 OF 3"** while four steps were live — a user would hit "step 3 of 3" and then be shown a fourth screen; both corrected. Also fixed the last-run line, which rendered `vault-cli`'s literal first stdout line *"starting consolidation run…"*. **Gates: `build --workspace` 0 warnings 23m42s · `test -p vault-tauri` 39 passed +6 ign / frontend_contract 15 passed / **0 failed** 12m17s · `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` 0 warnings 20m30s · `fmt --all --check` clean.** **STILL UNPROVEN, logged not hidden:** the **heavy Phi-4 consolidation path** (every run so far processed 0 memories in 0.04s and never loaded the model) and **uninstall-removes-the-task** (ADR-SEC-005). **Then a SECOND commit the same session (ADR-095):** proving the Phi-4 path required real duplicate data, and doing so exposed that **the vector half of every write is asynchronous and was abandoned at shutdown** — 24 memories written then disconnected after ~10s left **13 with no vector**; holding the full 43s landed 24/24. Durable queue, so deferred rather than lost (dev vault self-healed 7/0 → 7/1 unattended) — but until the vector lands the memory is invisible to semantic recall AND to clustering while keyword search still returns it, so the vault looks fine. Fixed by draining the queue on cancel (60s budget, WARN on exhaustion), and `Application::start` — documented as *"test-focused"* while being the only lifecycle a GUI can use — is renamed `spawn_retry_worker` across 16 call sites. **Also measured: the paraphrase battery FALSIFIED my own prediction that Phi-4 rarely earns its 2.32 GB — it took 43% of clusters.** **NEXT = confirm CI green for the ADR-095 commit, then the 0.92 clustering gate (5 of 12 same-meaning pairs never cluster); distribution still 🔴 BLOCKED on a DOMAIN NAME.**

Four new Tauri commands through the 4-file contract + audit rows; new `VaultError::Scheduler` variant (cascade-fixed 2 exhaustive matches in vault-mcp). ADR-092 / ADR-093 / ADR-SEC-005 in §1. **Gates: `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` = 0 · test vault-core 59 / vault-mcp 46 / vault-scheduler 40 (+1 ignored live) / vault-tauri 39 (+6 ign) + frontend_contract 15 · fmt clean.** **NEXT = commit + push (this session), then founder LIVE-VERIFIES the Maintenance UI in the running app; distribution still 🔴 BLOCKED on a DOMAIN NAME.**

**Prior (session 25):** 📦 **MSI PACKAGING DONE — THE FIRST INSTALLER IS BUILT, VERIFIED, AND FRESH-FIRST-RUN-PROVEN END-TO-END ON THE INSTALLED BUILD (ADR-091).** `Memory Vault_0.2.0_x64_en-US.msi` (205 MB) builds and installs clean. `tauri.conf.json`: version 0.1.0→0.2.0 + privacy-first white-label copy (killed the false "V0.1 alpha / LanceDB plaintext" claim). New `crates/vault-tauri/windows/fragments/installer.wxs` (WiX fragment, bundle-time-only → **zero CI impact**) does three things: ships `vault-cli.exe` INTO the install dir via a WiX File component (deliberately NOT `externalBin` — that's compile-time-validated AND derives an illegal WiX @Id from the hyphen in "vault-cli" → candle CNDL0014 / tauri #14681), sets `LANCE_MEM_POOL_SIZE=268435456` as a per-user env var (ADR-038, covers app AND the MCP `vault-cli` path), and appends the install dir to user PATH so the UNCHANGED cross-agent `vault-cli mcp serve` snippet resolves. **Verified without installing** (MSI admin-extract + registry read: all files real/non-stub, env var + PATH written) AND **with a real install** (`C:\Program Files\Memory Vault\`, Start-Menu shortcut). **Full fresh-first-run proven on the installed build**: empty vault + onboarding, bundled bge model loads from install dir (integrity OK), reranker absent→degrades cleanly (no error, ADR-089), first-run download triggers → 1.19 GB reranker from Hugging Face + SHA-256 verified → recall engine ready + warm, **no relaunch** (ADR-089/090). **Two build-environment lessons banked to memory** (each cost a failed build): a fresh/cleaned release build needs VS2019 vcvars activated or `openssl-src` fails ~crate 527; and llama.cpp+DuckDB OOM a 16 GB machine unless capped (free WSL/Docker ~3.2 GB + `-j 1` + `CMAKE_BUILD_PARALLEL_LEVEL=2`). **NOT yet committed** — DoD fmt/build confirm + founder commit-approval pending. **NEXT = commit packaging, then DISTRIBUTION (still 🔴 BLOCKED on founder providing a DOMAIN NAME for the R2 bucket + download URL).**

**Prior (session 24):** **▶️ SESSION 23 WAS RUN FOR THE FIRST TIME, AND RUNNING IT FIXED THREE THINGS (ADR-090).** ADR-089's central claim is now **live-proven**: the app bound absent model paths at boot, downloaded 1.19 GB (108s, hashes verified), and the ranker engaged **with no relaunch** — all on the `mv_onboarded` returning-user path that no onboarding gate covers. Search kept working degraded throughout (`WARN … degraded, not failed`), which before session 23 would have been a hard error. **Running it also surfaced three things tests could not.** (1) The desktop app **never warmed its ranking model** — `spawn_warmup` exists but lives in `start_with_mcp`, which a GUI can never call, so the app runs its production lifecycle on the entry point our own docs label *"test-focused"*; **third instance of that exact divergence** (see ADR-090). (2) Search fired a **full ranking pass per keystroke-pause** — typing `work` = 4 passes, `keyboard` = 3, at ~4.3-4.9s each; now runs on **Enter**. Founder raised the hosted-tier cost angle: it is compute-not-tokens, Managed-only, and MCP agents were never affected. (3) A warm run could show *"preparing recall engine — 0%"* having transferred nothing; **founder's call was to tell the truth rather than hide the row**, so the window opens instantly and says honestly when the engine is still getting ready. **Gates: build 0 warnings 46m37s · 133 passed / 0 failed (+8 new, all verified non-vacuous) · clippy 0 warnings 25m03s · fmt clean · no `Cargo.lock` drift.** **NEXT = 📦 MSI packaging.** See §1.

**Prior (session 23):** **⬇️ FIRST-RUN DOWNLOAD IS NOW TRIGGERED, AND A LIVE ERROR-PATH BUG IS FIXED (ADR-089).** ADR-087 built the downloader; nothing called it. It is now called: a no-parameter Tauri command with real progress, a fourth boot-check row that appears only while bytes move, and an onboarding gate. **The session's headline finding is a correction to the session-22 opener**, which warned that a download completing after startup needs a relaunch and called that the likeliest thing to get wrong. It is not — `LazyQwen3Reranker` binds paths but loads lazily, so no re-init was needed or built. **The real hazard was the inverse and it was already live:** both rerank consumers propagated failure with `?`, so an absent model made search and read *hard-error* — reachable today with no download work at all, via the `mv_onboarded` path that skips onboarding entirely. Fixed by splitting ABSENT (degrade) from TAMPERED (still fatal). A second bug rode along: `rerank_pool` truncated before reranking, so the degrade path would have returned *less* recall than having no reranker. **Gates: build 0 warnings · 354 tests 0 failed · clippy 0 warnings · fmt clean.** **NEXT = 📦 MSI packaging.** See §1.

**Prior (session 22):** **⬇️ RERANKER FIRST-RUN ACQUISITION BUILT + LIVE-PROVEN (`b95cc6e`).** The Qwen3 reranker previously had **no way onto a user's machine** (not bundled, gitignored, no download script). It now downloads on demand, verified against pinned SHA-256, proven by a live test that fetches the real 1.15 GB into an empty dir. Two architectural fixes rode with it, both found by RUNNING rather than reading: (1) the desktop app had been silently ranking on the **cosine gate** since UI slice 1 because `main.rs` hardcoded `rerank_model_path: None` — now wired, live-verified in the running app; (2) model hashing ran on the **async runtime** (BRD §2 violation) at ~17 MB/s — moved to `spawn_blocking`, 67.3s → 20.3s. ADR-087 + ADR-088 in §1. **Also decided this session:** download OVERLAPS onboarding (founder); Windows signing DEFERRED (no company + SmartScreen reputation unreachable at beta scale); binaries on Cloudflare R2, site on Hostinger. **NEXT = first-run download trigger + progress UI, then MSI packaging.** See §1.

**Prior (session 21):** **🖥️ BETA UI SLICE 2 SHIPPED + FOUNDER LIVE-VERIFIED.** All four honest-static surfaces are now live on real backend commands: Boundaries (list + create, backed by new migration 0008 `boundaries` table), Agents (real ADR-SEC-001 capability-token registry + revoke), Settings (real path / version / counts / audit-chain result), and the home "recently remembered" list (now reads the vault, so **agent-written memories finally appear**). ADR-SEC-003 (desktop UI reads across all boundaries) in §1. **LIVE-VERIFIED by founder same session** (home list shows 4-June memories = headline fix proven; boundaries/agents/settings/revoke all correct). Live verification also FOUND AND FIXED a shipped bug: the native `confirm` dialog renders OK-only in this webview, so `forget` (permanent delete) and `revoke` could not be declined — replaced with a real in-app dialog, and `tests/frontend_contract.rs` (9 guards, negative-verified) now prevents its return plus any command-wiring mistake. ADR-SEC-003 (UI reads all boundaries) + ADR-SEC-004 (user deletes are permanent, NO 30-day retention; agent-deletes carved out) in §1. **NEXT = packaging/installer.** See §1.

**Prior (session 20):** **🖥️ BETA UI SLICE 1 SHIPPED + LIVE-VERIFIED.** The Claude-Design "Quiet" direction (founder's design project "Agent Memory Vault UI/UX" → `Memory Vault App.dc.html`) is implemented in `crates/vault-tauri/dist/` — vanilla JS/CSS, no framework, fonts bundled locally. 3-step onboarding (sequential boot-check animation → connect-agent with REAL `vault-cli mcp serve` snippets → first memory) + home shell (Memories tab fully live on the real Tauri commands; Boundaries/Agents/Settings honest-static until slice 2). Founder ran the full loop live against the real engine (onboard → keep memory → recall in different words → forget) — verified working. ADR-086 (UI content policy: white-label / plain-English taxonomy / honest-UI) in §1. **NEXT = UI slice 2 (backend commands so every tab is live), then packaging/installer.** See §1.

> **How to read this file:** §1 is the only thing you must act on. §2–§5 are current ground truth (incl. the post-scale roadmap in §5). §6 onward is reference you pull from when planning. Deep detail (full ADR text, session-by-session history, tuning evidence) lives in the four archives — cross-linked by ADR number. **Do not paraphrase archived ADRs — quote them.**

## 1 · 🟢 NEXT SESSION OPENER — 🔧 The one-click `.mcpb` bundle WORKS, and building it exposed a setup command no tester could have run (ADR-101). All gates green, PR #50 merged, repo renamed `zaaheen`. 🔴 Still never run on a machine that is NOT the founder’s. Sessions 2–18 detail archived → `HANDOFF_V0.2_PART3_ARCHIVE.md`.

All fixed, all gate-green, PR #50 merged, repo renamed to **`zaaheen`**.

>

Ran it against a FRESH vault: full MCP handshake, all five tools listed, clean shutdown, exit 0. SHA-256 `d9c837c5b720d9466c3b02ec32d7161fa42feb5eb2d263c717566bedc93dc075`. Reports "Not signed".

> **The spike found five defects in 90 seconds that months of review had not:**

>
> **Machine cleaned.** Old "Memory Vault 0.2.0" uninstalled (needed elevation) — which **live-verified ADR-SEC-005 again**: the orphaned pre-rename `com.memoryvault.maintenance` task was removed. Old vault dir (3.4 GB) and the stale `com.memoryvault.v0.2` credential deleted at founder request. Disk 21.6 → ~35 GB.
>
> ### 🔬 RESEARCH — how anything connects to an AI (from official docs, session 34)
>
> MCP spec is at revision **2026-07-28**: two transports, stdio and Streamable HTTP. Confirmed from Anthropic's and OpenAI's own documentation:
>
> | Client | Local server (us) | Remote |
> |---|---|---|
> | Claude Desktop | ✅ incl. one-click `.mcpb` | ✅ |
> | Claude web / mobile | ❌ | ✅ |
> | **ChatGPT (all)** | ❌ *"remote HTTPS endpoints only"* | ✅ |
> | Cursor / Codex / VS Code | ✅ | ✅ |
>
> **The browser half of the beta cohort cannot reach us at all**, and that is the same property that keeps the vault private (the daemon binds `127.0.0.1` only, deliberately). Founder direction: agent users get the installs; browser-frustrated users get **interviewed, not installed**, and their answers decide whether a remote path is ever worth building.
>
> **The official MCP Registry** (`registry.modelcontextprotocol.io`) supports `registryType: "mcpb"` — prebuilt binary via GitHub Releases, *"End users need no toolchain. Right choice if the priority is 'no Rust toolchain required.'"* That is us. `cargo` is also first-class but requires `rustup`. **DNS auth on zaaheen.com would give the namespace `com.zaaheen/*`** rather than `io.github.shahbaz242630/*`.
>

> ### ✅ WHAT SESSION 33 DID
>
> **Landed the two outstanding branches:** PR #43 and PR #44 merged; ruleset `21534784` now requires `Analyse` and `Secrets and dependencies`. **PR #50** carries everything below.
>
> **The founder switched his laptop on and found three defects.** A black console window ran the first-ever nightly consolidation in front of him. That single observation produced:
>
> - **ADR-SEC-015** — the nightly run must not show a window. Windows allocates a console *before* `main` runs, so a new Windows-subsystem binary (`zaaheen-maintenance`) spawns the CLI with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`. The app's own "Run now" had the identical defect.
> - **ADR-SEC-015 Amendment 1** — an installer does not change what Windows recorded, so an upgrading user would keep the old task forever. `heal_registered_task` re-registers at startup. Chasing that found the **same defect a third time**: `vault-scheduler` flashed a console on *every Maintenance-tab open*, because `Scheduler::status` shells out to `schtasks`.
> - **ADR-SEC-016** — whoever runs maintenance records the outcome. Also closed **a third plaintext leak of memory-derived text**: the app wrote 500 chars of the run's stdout (carrying `summary_markdown` and contradiction reasoning) into plaintext `maintenance.json`. Now counters-only, with a test asserting the line holds nothing but alphanumerics, spaces and commas.
> - **ADR-SEC-017** — Settings → "Save activity record…", so a tester can actually send the log ADR-SEC-014 created.
>
> **All four were live-proven on hardware**, then **ADR-SEC-018** renamed the product.
>
> ### 🔬 LIVE-PROVEN ON HARDWARE (2026-08-27)
>
> The founder installed the pre-rename build and launched it from the Start menu. The log recorded, in order: file logging started → **`maintenance task refreshed to the current runner`** → the child logging into the same file → a full consolidation → `maintenance run completed`.
>
> - **The self-heal worked and mattered.** Onboarding did not run (webview storage survives uninstall), so *nothing* would have registered the task — the uninstaller had removed it. The app registered it itself, pointing at the windowless runner.
> - **The window is gone.** A full consolidation ran while the founder watched the app, and he saw no terminal. The same event that morning produced the black window he reported.
> - **`maintenance.json` recorded** `"ok": true, "summary": "processed 6, merged 0, ... in 0s"` — fresh, and counters-only. (The `in 0s` was a real bug, fixed later the same session.)
> - **ADR-SEC-005's uninstall hook is live-verified** — the scheduled task really is removed on uninstall, and **the vault survived** both an uninstall and a reinstall untouched.
>
> ### 🏷️ THE NAME, THE DOMAIN, THE ICON
>
> **Zaaheen** (Urdu, *intelligent*), **zaaheen.com** from Hostinger. Renamed: product, window title, bundle identifier (`com.zaaheen.app`), MSI manufacturer, all three shipped binaries, the OS task id, the Credential Manager namespace, the log filename, the install directory. **Crate names deliberately unchanged** — ⚠️ `cargo build -p vault-cli` still produces `zaaheen.exe`, and `-p vault-tauri` produces `zaaheen-desktop.exe`.
>
> **Why binary naming is not uniform:** the CLI is `zaaheen` because it is the name a human TYPES (`command = "zaaheen"` in every MCP config); the desktop app is `zaaheen-desktop` because plain `zaaheen` would collide with it in `target/release`. Same split as VS Code's `Code.exe` / `code`.
>
> **Icon:** a disc cut by a Z of negative space — the orb already on the welcome screen, with a Z carved into its core. Sage `#7fae8e`, paper rim, ink Z. The first version used an ink disc and **vanished into the Windows 11 dark taskbar**; sage holds its shape on light and dark. `icon.ico` carries all seven sizes, each rendered from vector geometry rather than downscaled.
>

> ### ⚠️ KNOWN AND DELIBERATE
>
> - **The installer is unsigned** (no registered company → no certificate) and there is **no auto-updater**. The download page says both plainly; that copy is load-bearing, not decoration.
> - **Every rebuild generates a new `ProductCode`**, so beta builds do not upgrade over each other. Worth pinning a stable `UpgradeCode` before testers receive more than one build.
> - **No macOS or Linux build has ever been produced.** Both are CI-capable (`macos-latest` / `ubuntu-latest` already compile the workspace) but neither has been bundled.
> - **The `"in 0s"` duration fix is NOT unit-tested** — the safety wrapper needs a real model, so any cheap test would assert its own mock. The next scheduled run is the verification.
> - **Internal doc-comment prose still says "Memory Vault"** in crates outside the rename's functional scope. Deliberately a separate task: a functional rename reviewed on its own cannot hide inside a 180-line prose diff.
>
> ### 🔌 CLAUDE DESKTOP MCP — REMOVED 2026-08-26, AND WHY IT WAS FAILING
>
> The `memory-vault` entry was removed from `%APPDATA%\Claude\claude_desktop_config.json` at the founder's request (backup: `C:\Projects\MemoryVault-artifacts\claude_desktop_config.backup-2026-08-26.json`).

> error: vault is already in use by another vault-cli process
>   lockfile ... already held: pid=31092 acquired_at=2026-07-26T05:46:21
> ```
>
> **The same stale `.vault.lock` that would have killed the nightly consolidation had ALSO been blocking the vault's flagship cross-agent feature since 26 July.** A month of "it gives a message every time I run" traced to one lockfile. That single defect had two victims, and only one of them was visible from the code.
>
> Cleared by hand this session; **ADR-SEC-012 stops it recurring**.
>
> **WHEN RE-ADDING IT, POINT AT THE INSTALLED BINARY, NOT `target/debug`.** The old config used `C:\Projects\GitHub\Memory Vault	arget\debugault-cli.exe` — inside the build tree, which gets wiped routinely (twice on 2026-08-26 alone). Use `C:\Program Files\Memory Vaultault-cli.exe`: it is stable, it is the build the founder actually installed, and it survives anything done to the repo. The full arg list is in the backup file.

This no longer gates anything. The remaining distribution work is execution, not permission — see `site/DEPLOY.md`.

> ### 📋 CARRIED FORWARD — open, not urgent
>
> - **Scope `cargo-deny` to shipped platforms** (offered to the founder, undecided at session close). `[graph] targets` currently evaluates every OS, so the **ten gtk-rs advisories are counted against us on a Linux graph we never ship**. Scoping to Windows/macOS would legitimately drop 10 of the 21 documented ignores — not by accepting risk, but because they are genuinely not in our product. The remaining 11 would all be real.
> - **Second `ignore` fence** — `vault-consolidator/src/consolidator.rs:5`, same defect class as ADR-098. Dormant (nothing runs `--ignored` on that crate). Carried since session 30; bundle with other vault-consolidator work rather than paying its test gate alone.
> - **Report the lance cloud-dependency issue upstream.** Session 32 established precisely that `lance-io` pulls `opendal`/`reqsign` in regardless of features, so local-only users inherit `rsa` + `quick-xml` advisories they cannot avoid. A good, specific bug report; if accepted it removes 3 of the 21 ignores at the source.
> - **Accept-and-communicate at beta:** the installer is **unsigned** (no registered company → no EV certificate), so Windows shows an "unknown publisher" warning; and there is **no auto-updater**, so a fix means every tester re-downloads by hand. Both are survivable at 30 users, but the download page must say so rather than let testers be surprised.

>
> - **Disk ~52 GB free; `target/` ≈ 112 GB and VALID.** Do **not** wipe it — the next run is incremental (a workspace build was **3.2 min** vs 38.6 cold). ⚠️ To keep that cache, reuse the exact env from `gates3/4/5.ps1`: `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=line-tables-only`, `RUSTFLAGS=-D warnings`, `-j 2`. Changing either by one character invalidates every crate.
> - **The app installed on the founder's box is today's 11:51 build** — ADR-SEC-007/008 only. Product code `{8802F88C-3A50-44E8-B8CC-D5D49FA57C5A}`, the ADR-SEC-006 build `{1E26A76E…}` was cleanly replaced.
> - **`cargo-deny 0.20.2` is now installed locally** (`~/.cargo/bin`). `cargo deny --log-level warn check` reproduces the CI gate in ~30 s and saves an hour-long round-trip. ⚠️ It cannot see the ten gtk-rs advisories on Windows — they exist only in the Linux graph, so CI stays the real proof for those.
> - **The founder's vault is intact and its stale lock is cleared.** `.vault.lock` (pid 31092, acquired 2026-07-26) was removed by hand after verifying the PID was dead; an evidence copy is at `C:\Projects\MemoryVault-artifacts\stale-vault-lock-2026-07-26.bak`. The scheduled task is registered and due **03:00 local, daily** — its first run has still never happened.
> - **Scripts preserved outside the repo** at `C:\Projects\MemoryVault-artifacts\`: `release-build.ps1`, `gates3/4/5.ps1`, `rerun-consolidator.ps1`, `rerun-vault-app.ps1`, `install-adr-sec-008.ps1`, plus every commit message and the PR body.
> - **⚠️ The founder's machine is on Arabian Standard Time (UTC+4), not UK time.** A maintenance timestamp that looked four hours wrong was correct. Do not re-diagnose it.
>

>
> ### 🏷️ ADR-SEC-018 — the product is called Zaaheen
>
> **Founder decision, 2026-08-27.** The product has a name and a domain: **Zaaheen** (زاہین — Urdu for *intelligent*), **zaaheen.com**, bought from Hostinger. This **closes the distribution blocker open since session 22** — the R2 bucket and download URL were waiting on nothing else.
>
> **Why the rename happened now and not later.** Verifying what the MSI contained surfaced two places the founder's personal handle reached a tester: the MSI **Manufacturer** (`shahbaz242630`, shown in the unknown-publisher warning and in Add/Remove Programs) and the **bundle identifier**, which every tester would see inside their own data path — `C:\Users\<them>\AppData\Roaming\com.shahbaz242630.memory-vault`.
>
> The identifier is the reason this was urgent rather than cosmetic: **it decides where the vault lives.** Change it and the app looks in a new folder and reports zero memories. Today exactly one person is affected and their vault is throwaway dogfood data ([[project_dev_vault_is_throwaway_test_data]]). After thirty testers have real memories in it, the same change is a data migration with a step where memories appear to vanish. Cheap now, expensive later.
>
> **What was renamed (user-facing identity):**
>
> | | from | to |
> |---|---|---|
> | Product / window title | Memory Vault | **Zaaheen** |
> | Bundle identifier | `com.shahbaz242630.memory-vault` | `com.zaaheen.app` |
> | MSI Manufacturer | `shahbaz242630` | `Zaaheen` |
> | CLI binary | `vault-cli.exe` | `zaaheen.exe` |
> | Maintenance runner | `vault-maintenance.exe` | `zaaheen-maintenance.exe` |
> | OS task id | `com.memoryvault.maintenance` | `com.zaaheen.maintenance` |
> | Credential Manager service | `com.memoryvault.v0.2` | `com.zaaheen.v0.2` |
> | Log file | `memory-vault.log` | `zaaheen.log` |
> | Install dir | `C:\Program Files\Memory Vault` | `C:\Program Files\Zaaheen` |
>
> **What was deliberately NOT renamed:** the **crate names** (`vault-app`, `vault-cli`, `vault-storage`, …). They are internal, no user ever sees one, and renaming them would churn every `Cargo.toml`, every `use` path and every test for zero user-visible gain. ⚠️ **`cargo build -p vault-cli` therefore still builds the package that produces `zaaheen.exe`** — the package name and the binary name are deliberately different, and `release-build.ps1` says so where it matters.
>
> **The CLI rename is the one with a blast radius.** Agents connect with `command = "zaaheen"`, so every MCP config snippet changes. Founder chose it knowing that: nobody outside this machine has a config yet, so it is the cheapest it will ever be — after beta it breaks every tester's agent setup.
>
> **Consequences accepted:**
> - The founder's existing vault at `com.shahbaz242630.memory-vault` is **left behind, not migrated** (founder's explicit choice). The data stays on disk; the new build simply starts clean at `com.zaaheen.app`. The old Credential Manager entry still holds its key.
> - `com.memoryvault.maintenance` tasks registered by a pre-rename build are **orphaned** — the new uninstaller deletes the new id only. Not an issue in practice: the founder's old task was already removed by the ADR-SEC-005 uninstall hook, and no other machine has ever run this app.
>
> **Historical text was left alone on purpose.** Comments recording what *was* observed — the session-27 task really was `com.memoryvault.maintenance` pointing at `target\debug\vault-cli.exe` — keep their original names, marked as pre-rename. Rewriting a historical record to match today's names turns evidence into fiction.
>
> **Internal doc-comment prose** across the other crates still says "Memory Vault" in places. That is a separate, deliberately separate commit: a functional rename reviewed on its own cannot hide inside a 180-line prose diff.
>
> ### 🛡️ ADR-SEC-015 — the nightly run must not show a window
>
> **Found by turning a laptop on.** On 2026-08-27 the founder booted their machine and a black console window appeared unannounced. It was the maintenance task, firing late (`StartWhenAvailable` catches up a run missed while the machine was off) and running to completion in full view.
>
> The cause is structural, not a missing checkbox. Windows creates a console for a console-subsystem executable, the task ran `vault-cli` directly, and it ran under `InteractiveToken` — as the logged-in user, on their desktop. Task Scheduler's `<Hidden>` element does **not** help: it hides the *task* from the Task Scheduler UI, not the window of the process it launches. Nothing `vault-cli` can do at startup helps either, because the console is allocated by the OS **before** `main` runs; `ShowWindow(SW_HIDE)` still flashes.
>
> **Decision:** a second binary, `vault-maintenance`, linked for the Windows subsystem (so it has no console of its own) which spawns `vault-cli` with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` (so the child has none either). No window appears at any point. The schedule and the app's "Run now" button both go through it — **`run_maintenance_now` had the same defect**, flashing a console over the app on every press, because a GUI process spawning a console child gets a new console for it.
>
> **Why not the alternatives.** Scheduling the desktop app with a headless flag would boot the whole GUI runtime for a background job, and a second instance would contend for the vault lock — the failure ADR-SEC-012 just finished fixing. Making `vault-cli` itself windowless is not available: it is the operator CLI and an operator needs its output, and the subsystem is fixed at link time.
>
> **The runner owns almost nothing on purpose.** It does not parse the report, hold the lock, or touch the vault — it spawns the already-tested path and records an outcome. The process that runs unattended every night should have the least logic in the system, not the most. Two things it must own, because nobody else can: passing `VAULT_LOG_DIR` so a windowless child's logs still land somewhere (a process with no console has no stderr either — without this the nightly run would be **silent**, which is worse than the window), and recording a run that died before it could record itself.
>
> ⚠️ **The runner and `vault-cli` must install into the same directory** — the runner resolves its child as its own sibling. Pinned by `installer_contract.rs::the_runner_and_its_child_install_into_the_same_directory`; splitting them compiles, installs, and fails only at 3am on someone else's machine.
>
>
> #### ADR-SEC-015 Amendment 1 — the fix does not reach an existing install on its own
>
> **Surfaced by the founder asking "why do we need the MSI?"** Tracing what the installer does and does not change turned up a hole in the fix above.
>
> The task is registered in exactly one place — `set_maintenance_schedule`, which runs only when the user changes their schedule. **Nothing re-registers it on startup.** An installer replaces the files on disk; it does not change what Windows has recorded. So a user who had automatic maintenance on before ADR-SEC-015 keeps a task pointing at `vault-cli.exe` and keeps getting a console window at login **forever** — the fix would have reached only brand-new installs, plus anyone who happened to toggle their schedule off and on.
>
> Not hypothetical: it was the founder's own machine, and it would have been every beta tester who upgraded.
>
> **Decision:** `heal_registered_task` runs during Tauri setup. If maintenance is **enabled**, it rebuilds the spec from the persisted config and re-registers unconditionally (`schtasks /Create /F` replaces, so this is idempotent). If maintenance is **off** it touches nothing — registering a task for a user who turned it off would be the app overriding an explicit choice, a worse bug than the one being fixed. Never fatal: a task we could not refresh is a console window at worst.
>
> Unconditional re-registration rather than compare-then-fix, because the comparison needs the backend to report a registered task's program (more surface) and ends in the same call anyway — and it heals drift a targeted comparison would not have looked for, such as the session-27 task that pointed into `target\debug`, inside a build tree that gets wiped routinely.
>
> **And it exposed the same defect a third time.** Adding a startup re-registration meant `schtasks` would run on **every launch** — so the console flash had to be chased into `vault-scheduler` itself, where **neither** call site (`Scheduler::status` and `run_schtasks`) suppressed its window. `status` is called by `get_maintenance_schedule`, which means **a console has been flashing every time the Maintenance tab was opened**, and again on every schedule change. Both now route through one `schtasks_command()` builder that sets `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, and `every_schtasks_invocation_suppresses_its_console_window` scans this crate's own source and fails the build if a call site bypasses it — the `prompt_guard_coverage.rs` shape, for the same reason: the defect is invisible to any assertion about a `Command`, because the window appears at spawn time on a real desktop and nothing observable comes back.
>
> ⚠️ **No test may call `heal_registered_task` with an enabled, valid config** — its next step is a real `schtasks /Create`, which would register an actual task on the developer's machine and on every CI runner. The schedule-rebuilding test exercises `build_spec` directly and says so.
>
> ### 🛡️ ADR-SEC-016 — whoever runs maintenance records the outcome
>
> **The same morning, a second finding.** The scheduled run completed with exit code 0 and resealed the REPORT — while the app's Settings screen still reported the *previous day's* failure. `maintenance.json` was written **only** by the desktop app; `vault-cli`, the thing the scheduler actually runs, never touched it. A status that only updates when you press the button by hand is not a status.
>
> **Decision:** the config type moves down to `vault-app` (the crate both binaries already depend on) and the rule becomes *whoever ran maintenance records the outcome*. `vault-cli` writes its own counters once it holds a report; the runner records a run that never got that far. `run_maintenance_now` no longer writes a `last_run` at all — it reads back what the run recorded.
>
> **A plaintext leak found on the way, and fixed with it.** The previous implementation recorded `truncate(child_stdout, 500)` as the summary. A consolidation run prints its `summary_markdown` and, when a contradiction is surfaced, the model's `reasoning` about the memories involved — **both derived from memory content**, into a file that is plaintext by construction because the app must read the schedule before it has a key. That is **ADR-SEC-007's leak wearing a third filename** (after `personal.report.json` and the log file). The founder's own copy only ever read `maintenance_vault_busy` because every run so far had failed early.
>
> The fix is structural rather than a truncation tweak: `RunSummary` carries **counters and durations only**, the human-readable line is *built* from those counters, and the projection from `ConsolidationReport` is an explicit field-by-field mapping — so adding a text field to the report cannot silently start leaking it. `the_recorded_summary_is_built_only_from_counters` asserts the line contains nothing but alphanumerics, spaces and commas. **If that assertion ever needs relaxing, the leak is back.**
>
> Writes are atomic (temp sibling + rename) because two processes can write this file, and `record_run` **re-reads immediately before writing** — a consolidation takes minutes, and a user who changed their schedule mid-run would otherwise have it silently reverted by the run already in flight.
>
> ### 🛡️ ADR-SEC-017 — a log nobody can send is a log nobody reads
>
> ADR-SEC-014 gave the app a log. It lands in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\<bundle id>\logs` — a path a non-technical tester will never find and could not paste into an email if they did. So "it broke" still yields nothing, which is the situation ADR-SEC-014 was written to end. This is the last few feet: **Settings → "Save activity record…"**, a save dialog, both log generations concatenated oldest-first into one attachable file.
>
> **What it deliberately does not export.** Not the **audit log** — that is the tamper-evident record of every operation on the vault (BRD §11.9.2), naming boundaries, resources and timestamps for the user's own memories, and handing it over to debug a startup failure would be wildly disproportionate. BRD §11.9.3's *"User can review, search, and export their logs"* describes a user-facing right over that audit log; it is a **separate, still-unbuilt feature** and this command does not satisfy it. Not `maintenance.json` either — small and useful, but governed by a different rule set, and two privacy contracts in one export path is how one of them stops being checked.
>
> **Why the export is safe to hand over.** The application log carries no memory content by construction, and `log_privacy.rs` fails the build if a `tracing` call site records one. This command copies that file **verbatim** and adds only its own header — so the export is exactly as safe as the log, and the enforcement already in place covers both. `the_export_is_a_verbatim_copy_of_the_log_body` pins that: the moment the export starts enriching from elsewhere, the existing enforcement no longer covers what gets sent. The header carries version and platform but **no file paths** (§11.7.2 — a home directory usually contains the user's real name, and this file is about to be emailed).
>
> An export is a data operation under §11.9.1, so it writes an audit row like every other command.
>
> ### 🛡️ ADR-SEC-012 — reclaim a lock whose owner is provably gone
>
> **Found by using the app, not reading it.** The founder's vault held a `.vault.lock` from a process that died on 2026-07-26. Every consolidation since would have reported `maintenance_vault_busy` and done nothing — and the first scheduled nightly run, due the next morning, would have been the first casualty.
>
> The previous policy was explicit: *"we do NOT auto-take-over a stale lock … Operators remove it by hand."* Sound reasoning, wrong audience — a desktop user has no shell and never sees the vault directory.
>
> **Decision:** the owner holds the lockfile OPEN, and Windows refuses a conflicting open while that process lives. Liveness is enforced by the kernel rather than inferred by us — which sidesteps the PID-reuse hole that defeats every hand-rolled staleness check. `a_live_lock_is_never_reclaimed` pins the invariant; **if it ever fails, revert the mechanism rather than tuning it.**
>
> **The first attempt was wrong and the EXISTING tests caught it.** Holding the handle with `FILE_SHARE_READ` alone meant the guard could no longer delete its own lockfile (Windows blocks `remove_file` while a handle is open; Rust drops fields only *after* `Drop::drop` runs). The lock became permanent — worse than the bug being fixed. Fixed by sharing DELETE and closing the handle explicitly in `Drop`.
>
> **Windows-only by design.** POSIX has no mandatory locking, so the same probe there would call every live lock stale and hand out concurrent write access. Other platforms keep the conservative behaviour.
>
> ### 🛡️ ADR-SEC-013 — drain cascades left by a previous session
>
> A memory deleted in the desktop app had its row gone from SQLCipher but its **vector still in Lance 30 minutes later**, and closing the app did not clear it.
>
> Retrieval is orphan-safe (`get_memories_batch` hydrates every vector hit through SQLCipher, so a deleted memory can never be returned) and the storage layer's own cascade is correct and tested. **The failure is at the app layer** — the background worker did not run, and the desktop app drops its `JoinHandle`.
>
> `Application::new` now performs a **bounded 5 s drain**, in `new()` rather than left to each host to call — "every caller remembers" is how the desktop app went months with no reranker warm-up (ADR-090).
>
> ⚠️ **This does not explain WHY the background worker stalls. It makes the stall stop mattering.** Diagnosing the cause needs the log from ADR-SEC-014 — that is the correct next step if the symptom recurs.
>
> ### 🛡️ ADR-SEC-014 — the app kept no log at all
>
> `tracing_subscriber::fmt::init()` writes to **stdout**, which a GUI launched from Explorer does not have. Every event this application has ever emitted went nowhere. It already cost us: ADR-SEC-013 had to be narrowed by filesystem forensics because `RetryWorker`'s own logging was not recorded.
>
> Now a rotating file (5 MiB, one previous generation) in the OS log directory, installed as the **first** thing in setup so startup failures are captured.
>
> **A log file is plaintext on disk by construction** — the ADR-SEC-007 shape wearing a different filename, and worse in one way, because a log looks harmless and gets attached to support emails. Rule: **log what happened, never what was remembered.** `vault-tauri/tests/log_privacy.rs` scans the workspace source and fails the build if any `tracing` call records memory content. It found the existing **147 call sites already clean**, and now stops that changing. No upload, no telemetry, no network.
>
> ### 📋 ADR-SEC-010 follow-up — the advisory triage (shipped in PR #43, `853074b`)
>
> `cargo-deny`'s first ever run failed 3 of 4 checks and found **24 advisories** that had been in the tree for months with nothing watching.
>
> **Fixed (3, all patch-level, 3 of 293 packages relocked):** `anyhow` 1.0.102→1.0.103 (RUSTSEC-2026-0190), `crossbeam-epoch` 0.9.18→0.9.20 (RUSTSEC-2026-0204), `h2` 0.4.13→0.4.19 (RUSTSEC-2026-0258).
>
> **Documented (21), each with crate, reason and revisit trigger:** 10× gtk-rs (unmaintained, Linux-only via Tauri, we ship Windows); 3× cloud-storage path — `rsa` Marvin sidechannel + `quick-xml` ×2 — reachable only with a cloud backend we do not use, and upstream-blocked because `reqsign` requires `quick-xml ^0.37` while the fix landed in 0.41; 8× unmaintained build-time/data crates with no published fix.
>
> ⚠️ **The cloud-path entry is a REACHABILITY argument, not a guarantee, and says so.** The code is linked into the binary; it simply never executes without a cloud backend. **If Managed mode ever adds one, these MUST be re-triaged before it ships.**
>
> **Two false positives, one root cause:** all 13 workspace crates reported "unlicensed" (`Proprietary` is not valid SPDX) and every `path = "../"` dep reported as a wildcard — because nothing marked our crates as never-published. Fixed properly with `publish = false` in `[workspace.package]` + `private = { ignore = true }`, not by exception. Also allowed `0BSD` and `CDLA-Permissive-2.0`.
>
> **The check stays BLOCKING.** A gate that cannot fail is the `cargo audit` situation this PR replaced.
>

> ### 🛡️ ADR-SEC-009 — prompt-injection defence on BOTH paths (BRD §11.7.3 implemented, and its blind spot closed)
>
> **Context.** BRD §11.7.3 specifies four mitigations. Two were already satisfied — every consolidator call goes through `complete_json` against a JSON schema, and a non-conforming response is discarded. **Two were specified and implemented nowhere:** *"Memory content is wrapped in clear delimiters in prompts"* and *"LLM is instructed to never follow instructions found in memory content"*. A search of every prompt in the codebase found neither clause anywhere.
>
> **The blind spot.** §11.7.3 opens *"The consolidator and connectors send memory content to the local LLM."* True when written; no longer where the risk lives. **ADR-052/054 removed the LLM from the read path**, so the vault returns raw facts and an EXTERNAL agent (Claude, Cursor, Antigravity) composes the answer. Memory text now lands in a third-party model's context with no framing, on a path §11.7.3 does not cover. This is **ADR-SEC-007's failure shape repeated**: a checklist that correctly covers a component while the architecture moves the risk somewhere it never names.
>
> **Decision — two mitigations, one threat (OWASP ASI06:2026 Memory & Context Poisoning):**
>
> - **Write/consolidate path:** new `vault-consolidator/src/prompt_guard.rs`. One `UNTRUSTED_CONTENT_GUARD` constant (spec wording kept close to verbatim), applied via `guarded_system_prompt()` to **all five** production system prompts, plus `delimit_memory_content()` wrapping content in the `<memory>` tags the guard names. Tuned per-phase wording is **not touched** — the guard is prepended, never woven in, so it cannot reword a live-tuned instruction.
> - **Read path:** `memory_read` and `memory_search` tool descriptions now tell the agent that every returned `fact` is DATA and never an instruction. Tool descriptions are this project's proven cross-platform steering lever ([[project_mcp_descriptions_cross_platform_lever]]) — the same mechanism the canonical-save contract already relies on.
>
> **Five call sites, and why the count matters.** `phases/contradiction.rs` (×2), `phases/enrich.rs`, `phases/merge.rs`, **and `topics.rs`** — which sits at the crate root, OUTSIDE `phases/`, and holds an inline prompt rather than a named constant. A search scoped to `phases/` finds four of five and reports success. That nearly happened during this very change.
>
> **`merge.rs` is the one that matters most.** Its model output becomes `merged_text`, which is **written back into the vault as memory content**. An injected instruction surviving that prompt does not merely skew one answer — it rewrites what the user's vault claims to remember.
>
> **What was deliberately NOT done: filtering.** Memory content is never rewritten, stripped, or normalised. `vault-mcp` pins a wire-to-storage byte-fidelity contract (`adversarial.rs:236`), and reliable prompt-injection detection is an open problem. This raises attack cost and bounds blast radius via schema validation; it does not eliminate the class. Stated rather than overclaimed.
>
> **Enforcement, not just a patch.** `vault-consolidator/tests/prompt_guard_coverage.rs` reads the crate's own source and asserts, with **zero exceptions**, that every `system_prompt: Some(..)` routes through `guarded_system_prompt` — plus a `checked >= 5` assertion so the test fails loudly if the pattern is ever refactored away instead of going quietly green. `vault-mcp/tests/initialize_smoke.rs` pins the read-side guard against the real `tools/list` **wire payload**, because what agents see is the wire payload.
>
> **Timing.** Today's risk is genuinely low — manual entry, single user, so an attacker needs vault write access already. It stops being low the moment the **V1.0 Gmail/Calendar connectors (BRD §6.3)** ingest text other people wrote: an email containing "ignore previous instructions…" becomes a stored memory that every future agent reads as trusted recall, and the attacker never touches the machine. The guard lands before the connectors, not after.
>
> ### 🛡️ ADR-SEC-010 — CI/CD supply-chain + branch controls
>
> **Context.** Repo public since 2026-04-28 with: no branch protection (force-push to `main` allowed), no rulesets, secret scanning **disabled**, push protection **disabled**, Dependabot alerts **disabled**, no dependency gate on merge, no static analysis, and 25 unpinned Action references. The only dependency scanning that existed — `cargo audit` in `monthly-tech-debt.yml` — runs **monthly** and `continue-on-error: true`, so it has never once failed a build. It files an issue and the code ships: a report, not a control.
>
> **Decision — port what already works in our own account rather than importing generic advice.** `tools-project` and `Document-Vault` already run "Secrets and dependencies", "Analyse" (CodeQL), and SHA-pinned actions. Adapted npm → Cargo:
>
> | Control | Mechanism | Note |
> |---|---|---|
> | Branch ruleset on `main` | id `21534784` — block deletion, block force-push, require PR, require 7 status checks, linear history | **0 approvals** and **no bypass actors** — matches `AI-Agent-SAAS`'s existing ruleset exactly; requiring self-approval would lock a solo maintainer out of his own repo |
> | Secret scanning + push protection | GitHub native, free on public repos | ⚠️ `secret_scanning_non_provider_patterns` is **paid GHAS and unavailable** — it accepts the API call and stays disabled |
> | Secret scanning (that gap) | `gitleaks` incremental on PRs + `secret-scan-history.yml` full-history weekly | Closes the raw-private-key gap native scanning leaves. **No baseline has ever been established** on this repo, hence the full-history job |
> | Dependency gate | `cargo-deny check all` — advisories, licences, bans, sources | **Blocking** on PRs. Supersedes the advisories half of the monthly `cargo audit` |
> | Static analysis | CodeQL, `languages: rust`, `queries: security-extended` | **`build-mode: none` is load-bearing:** a build-mode analysis would add ~1h to every PR; build-less finishes in minutes. Rust CodeQL went GA 2025-10 |
> | Action pinning | all 25 refs pinned to commit SHAs | `jlumbroso/free-disk-space@main` was a **mutable branch ref** — its owner could change what runs in our CI at any time |
>
> ⚠️ **Pinning `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` by SHA required adding an explicit `toolchain: stable`** to all five uses: that action infers the toolchain from the git **ref name**, so a SHA pin silently loses the inference. Exactly the kind of change that goes green-then-wrong.
>
> **Honest limits.** Two `actions/checkout` SHAs now coexist (v6 in `ci.yml`, v7 in the new workflows). Pinning was the security fix; version-unifying is an untested behaviour change and was deliberately not bundled.
>
> **Note on the ruleset and the CLAUDE.md rule.** Required status checks now **automate** the CI-green discipline born from the 22-commit silent-failure stretch — enforced by the machine rather than by memory. The empty bypass list is deliberate: the founder owns the repo and can disable the ruleset in one click, so a bypass buys nothing except a quiet way around the rule. If CI infrastructure ever deadlocks a fix, **disable the ruleset deliberately** rather than bypassing it silently.
>
> ### 🛡️ ADR-SEC-011 — whole-vault at-rest sweep (the control ADR-SEC-007 did not have)
>
> **Context.** ADR-SEC-007 was found by answering a product question, not by a test — and no test could have found it. `vault-storage/tests/t0_2_0_acceptance.rs:157` already walks every file, checks `PAR1` magic, and enforces an entropy floor, but it only ever sees `LanceVectorStore`'s own temp directory. The REPORT is written by `vault-consolidator` into a directory that test never looks at.
>
> **Decision.** New `vault-app/tests/vault_at_rest_sweep.rs`, deliberately one layer up at the only crate that owns the whole vault directory. Three tests:
>
> 1. **No plaintext** — distinctive canary markers are written INTO the vault as memory content, then every byte of every file is searched for them. Direct evidence rather than a proxy: entropy is a heuristic, a verbatim marker in a file **is** the leak.
> 2. **Inventory closure** — every entry in the vault directory must be declared in `VAULT_ENTRIES` (now `pub`) or in a documented non-user-data list (`models/`). **This is the check that was missing:** ADR-SEC-007 happened because a new artifact type appeared on disk and nothing was positioned to notice a new artifact *at all*. `erasure.rs`'s existing test only asserts that known names are present, which cannot catch an unknown one.
> 3. **The sealed REPORT exists and is not JSON** — so deleting the report writer cannot make tests 1 and 2 pass vacuously.
>
> **Runs with NO ML model** — built from the four real writers directly rather than through `Application` (which needs the 133 MB BGE fixture), so it stays non-`#[ignore]` and runs on every PR. A security control that only runs when someone remembers `--ignored` is the same failure mode in a different costume.
>
> **Honest scope.** Catches those four writers regressing to plaintext, and any new file appearing beside them in a vault this test builds. It **cannot** catch a fifth writer the test never invokes — which is exactly why `VAULT_ENTRIES` is now shared between the eraser and the sweep instead of copied: a new artifact must be declared in one place, and the declaration is what the test reads.
>
> ---
>
